People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Maybe a little more—like two years and a couple months. It kind of started after the clinic staffing changed and my schedule started rotating all over the place. I kept thinking I’d adjust, you know, but… it just sort of stayed.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I guess some things don’t feel the same, but I don’t know if I’d say I don’t enjoy anything anymore. Maybe I just don’t get into stuff as easily as I used to. Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m… honestly not sure how to answer that. I don’t think there’s one specific thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more like I don’t get as excited about stuff, maybe? But I can still enjoy things sometimes, I think. I don’t want to overstate it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. Like… I still do some things, and sometimes they’re okay. It’s more that I don’t feel as pulled toward them or as excited as I used to, maybe. I’m not sure if that counts as losing interest, though.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say unable, exactly. That sounds kind of… strong. I mean, I can still have moments where something feels nice or I laugh at something. It’s just not always as much as I’d expect, I guess. But I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things that used to make you happy no longer bring you any happiness at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “no happiness at all,” no. That feels a little too absolute. I mean, some things still feel… okay, or I can get a little enjoyment from them. It’s just kind of muted sometimes, I guess. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything significant that I’ve really noticed. My weight’s been kind of… normal for me, I guess. I haven’t been tracking it closely, though, so I’m not sure if that’s the kind of answer you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods in the past two years where you felt significantly better than you do now?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, maybe there were days here and there that felt a little easier, but I don’t think I’ve had a real stretch where I felt significantly better. It’s been going on for about two years and maybe two months now, since the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule started rotating all over the place. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where your low mood was significantly worse than it typically is?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not sure if I’d call them “episodes,” exactly. It’s more like the low feeling is kind of there most of the time. I wake up already feeling down, like there’s this heavy gray feeling on me before I even get to the clinic, and it sort of follows me through the day.\n\nSome days feel heavier than others, I guess, but it’s not like there are clear separate episodes. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to look forward to things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I still try to do things if I’m supposed to, or if someone invites me. But looking forward to them… maybe not the same way? I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. It’s just kind of hard to tell sometimes whether I’m not excited, or I’m just tired or distracted.\n\nDoes that make sense, or should I explain it differently?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I’m not sure if that counts as losing interest, though.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say unable, exactly. That sounds kind of… strong. I mean, I can still have moments where something feels nice or I laugh at something. It’s just not always as much as I’d expect, I guess. But I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things that used to make you happy no longer bring you any happiness at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “no happiness at all,” no. That feels a little too absolute. I mean, some things still feel… okay, or I can get a little enjoyment from them. It’s just kind of muted sometimes, I guess. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything significant that I’ve really noticed. My weight’s been kind of… normal for me, I guess. I haven’t been tracking it closely, though, so I’m not sure if that’s the kind of answer you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods in the past two years where you felt significantly better than you do now?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, maybe there were days here and there that felt a little easier, but I don’t think I’ve had a real stretch where I felt significantly better. It’s been going on for about two years and maybe two months now, since the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule started rotating all over the place. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where your low mood was significantly worse than it typically is?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not sure if I’d call them “episodes,” exactly. It’s more like the low feeling is kind of there most of the time. I wake up already feeling down, like there’s this heavy gray feeling on me before I even get to the clinic, and it sort of follows me through the day.\n\nSome days feel heavier than others, I guess, but it’s not like there are clear separate episodes. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to look forward to things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I still try to do things if I’m supposed to, or if someone invites me. But looking forward to them… maybe not the same way? I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. It’s just kind of hard to tell sometimes whether I’m not excited, or I’m just tired or distracted.\n\nDoes that make sense, or should I explain it differently?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to force yourself to do things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I think… sometimes, yeah. Not always in a big way, but there are things I know I used to enjoy, and now I kind of have to talk myself into doing them.\n\nLike, if I do go along with something, I can get through it and I don’t want people to think I’m being difficult. But the starting part feels harder than it should. I don’t know if that counts as forcing myself exactly, but it feels like I’m pushing myself more than I used to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. patients and catching up on charts, it feels like I have nothing left to do laundry, shoot hoops, or even mess with my electronics stuff.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: I mean, I still get up and go to the clinic and everything, but most mornings I wake up and it’s already there—like this heavy gray feeling on me before the day even starts. And it kind of follows me around all day. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about two years now. Maybe a little more—like two years and a couple months. It kind of started after the clinic staffing changed and my schedule started rotating all over the place. I kept thinking I’d adjust, you know, but… it just sort of stayed.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I guess some things don’t feel the same, but I don’t know if I’d say I don’t enjoy anything anymore. Maybe I just don’t get into stuff as easily as I used to. Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m… honestly not sure how to answer that. I don’t think there’s one specific thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more like I don’t get as excited about stuff, maybe? But I can still enjoy things sometimes, I think. I don’t want to overstate it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. It feels more continuous, I think. Not like, “Oh, this week was an episode and then I was back to normal.” It’s more that I wake up already feeling low—kind of that heavy gray feeling I mentioned—and it follows me through most of the day.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F33.0, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess I’ve just been feeling pretty low for a long time now. I mean, I still get up and go to the clinic and everything, but most mornings I wake up and it’s already there—like this heavy gray feeling on me before the day even starts. And it kind of follows me around all day. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about two years now. Maybe a little more—like two years and a couple months. It kind of started after the clinic staffing changed and my schedule started rotating all over the place. I kept thinking I’d adjust, you know, but… it just sort of stayed.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I guess some things don’t feel the same, but I don’t know if I’d say I don’t enjoy anything anymore. Maybe I just don’t get into stuff as easily as I used to. Is that kind of what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but no longer enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m… honestly not sure how to answer that. I don’t think there’s one specific thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more like I don’t get as excited about stuff, maybe? But I can still enjoy things sometimes, I think. I don’t want to overstate it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. Like… I still do some things, and sometimes they’re okay. It’s more that I don’t feel as pulled toward them or as excited as I used to, maybe. I’m not sure if that counts as losing interest, though.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say unable, exactly. That sounds kind of… strong. I mean, I can still have moments where something feels nice or I laugh at something. It’s just not always as much as I’d expect, I guess. But I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things that used to make you happy no longer bring you any happiness at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “no happiness at all,” no. That feels a little too absolute. I mean, some things still feel… okay, or I can get a little enjoyment from them. It’s just kind of muted sometimes, I guess. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything significant that I’ve really noticed. My weight’s been kind of… normal for me, I guess. I haven’t been tracking it closely, though, so I’m not sure if that’s the kind of answer you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods in the past two years where you felt significantly better than you do now?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, maybe there were days here and there that felt a little easier, but I don’t think I’ve had a real stretch where I felt significantly better. It’s been going on for about two years and maybe two months now, since the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule started rotating all over the place. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where your low mood was significantly worse than it typically is?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not sure if I’d call them “episodes,” exactly. It’s more like the low feeling is kind of there most of the time. I wake up already feeling down, like there’s this heavy gray feeling on me before I even get to the clinic, and it sort of follows me through the day.\n\nSome days feel heavier than others, I guess, but it’s not like there are clear separate episodes. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to look forward to things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I still try to do things if I’m supposed to, or if someone invites me. But looking forward to them… maybe not the same way? I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. It’s just kind of hard to tell sometimes whether I’m not excited, or I’m just tired or distracted.\n\nDoes that make sense, or should I explain it differently?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to force yourself to do things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I think… sometimes, yeah. Not always in a big way, but there are things I know I used to enjoy, and now I kind of have to talk myself into doing them.\n\nLike, if I do go along with something, I can get through it and I don’t want people to think I’m being difficult. But the starting part feels harder than it should. I don’t know if that counts as forcing myself exactly, but it feels like I’m pushing myself more than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low moods come and go in any kind of pattern, like weekly, monthly, or seasonally?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s really weekly or monthly, at least not that I’ve noticed. It’s been more… just ongoing. I guess it’s been about two years now—maybe a little over, like two years and a couple months.\n\nIt kind of started after the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule began rotating all over the place. I don’t know if that’s the kind of pattern you mean, but that’s when it seemed to settle in and not really let up.\n\nDoctor: Is this low feeling something that you experience for most of the day, almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up already feeling low, like there’s this heavy gray feeling on me before I even get to the clinic. And it kind of stays with me through the day, more or less.\n\nIs that what you mean by most of the day?\n\nDoctor: Do you think of your low periods as separate episodes, or is it more of a continuous feeling?\n\nPatient: It feels more continuous, I think. Not like, “Oh, this week was an episode and then I was back to normal.” It’s more that I wake up already feeling low—kind of that heavy gray feeling I mentioned—and it follows me through most of the day.\n\nI mean, I can still function and get to the clinic, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but… yeah, it’s been more ongoing than separate periods, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t think there’s one specific thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more like I don’t get as excited about stuff, maybe? But I can still enjoy things sometimes, I think. I don’t want to overstate it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. Like… I still do some things, and sometimes they’re okay. It’s more that I don’t feel as pulled toward them or as excited as I used to, maybe. I’m not sure if that counts as losing interest, though.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say unable, exactly. That sounds kind of… strong. I mean, I can still have moments where something feels nice or I laugh at something. It’s just not always as much as I’d expect, I guess. But I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things that used to make you happy no longer bring you any happiness at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “no happiness at all,” no. That feels a little too absolute. I mean, some things still feel… okay, or I can get a little enjoyment from them. It’s just kind of muted sometimes, I guess. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything significant that I’ve really noticed. My weight’s been kind of… normal for me, I guess. I haven’t been tracking it closely, though, so I’m not sure if that’s the kind of answer you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods in the past two years where you felt significantly better than you do now?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, maybe there were days here and there that felt a little easier, but I don’t think I’ve had a real stretch where I felt significantly better. It’s been going on for about two years and maybe two months now, since the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule started rotating all over the place. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where your low mood was significantly worse than it typically is?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not sure if I’d call them “episodes,” exactly. It’s more like the low feeling is kind of there most of the time. I wake up already feeling down, like there’s this heavy gray feeling on me before I even get to the clinic, and it sort of follows me through the day.\n\nSome days feel heavier than others, I guess, but it’s not like there are clear separate episodes. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to look forward to things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I still try to do things if I’m supposed to, or if someone invites me. But looking forward to them… maybe not the same way? I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. It’s just kind of hard to tell sometimes whether I’m not excited, or I’m just tired or distracted.\n\nDoes that make sense, or should I explain it differently?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to force yourself to do things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I think… sometimes, yeah. Not always in a big way, but there are things I know I used to enjoy, and now I kind of have to talk myself into doing them.\n\nLike, if I do go along with something, I can get through it and I don’t want people to think I’m being difficult. But the starting part feels harder than it should. I don’t know if that counts as forcing myself exactly, but it feels like I’m pushing myself more than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low moods come and go in any kind of pattern, like weekly, monthly, or seasonally?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s really weekly or monthly, at least not that I’ve noticed. It’s been more… just ongoing. I guess it’s been about two years now—maybe a little over, like two years and a couple months.\n\nIt kind of started after the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule began rotating all over the place. I don’t know if that’s the kind of pattern you mean, but that’s when it seemed to settle in and not really let up.\n\nDoctor: Is this low feeling something that you experience for most of the day, almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up already feeling low, like there’s this heavy gray feeling on me before I even get to the clinic. And it kind of stays with me through the day, more or less.\n\nIs that what you mean by most of the day?\n\nDoctor: Do you think of your low periods as separate episodes, or is it more of a continuous feeling?\n\nPatient: It feels more continuous, I think. Not like, “Oh, this week was an episode and then I was back to normal.” It’s more that I wake up already feeling low—kind of that heavy gray feeling I mentioned—and it follows me through most of the day.\n\nI mean, I can still function and get to the clinic, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but… yeah, it’s been more ongoing than separate periods, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this low for most of every day over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. It’s been going on for about two years and maybe a couple months now.\n\nI mean, I can still get through the day, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but the low feeling has been there most days, for most of the day, since around when the clinic staffing changed and my schedule started rotating all over the place. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt low for a shorter amount of time than you do now?\n\nPatient: Not really shorter, no… or at least I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s more like it’s been this same ongoing thing for about two years and two months, since the clinic staffing changed and my schedule started rotating all over the place. I guess there are better moments here and there, but I wouldn’t say there were separate shorter low periods, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not for a full two months, anyway.\n\nThere have been days or maybe a week where I’m a little better, but this has pretty much been going on for about two years and two months, since the clinic staffing changed and my schedule started rotating all over the place. So, no, I don’t think I’ve had a real two-month break from it. Does that answer it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

But I don’t know if that’s the same thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things that used to make you happy no longer bring you any happiness at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s “no happiness at all,” no. That feels a little too absolute. I mean, some things still feel… okay, or I can get a little enjoyment from them. It’s just kind of muted sometimes, I guess. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything significant that I’ve really noticed. My weight’s been kind of… normal for me, I guess. I haven’t been tracking it closely, though, so I’m not sure if that’s the kind of answer you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods in the past two years where you felt significantly better than you do now?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, maybe there were days here and there that felt a little easier, but I don’t think I’ve had a real stretch where I felt significantly better. It’s been going on for about two years and maybe two months now, since the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule started rotating all over the place. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes where your low mood was significantly worse than it typically is?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not sure if I’d call them “episodes,” exactly. It’s more like the low feeling is kind of there most of the time. I wake up already feeling down, like there’s this heavy gray feeling on me before I even get to the clinic, and it sort of follows me through the day.\n\nSome days feel heavier than others, I guess, but it’s not like there are clear separate episodes. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to look forward to things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I still try to do things if I’m supposed to, or if someone invites me. But looking forward to them… maybe not the same way? I don’t want to make it sound dramatic. It’s just kind of hard to tell sometimes whether I’m not excited, or I’m just tired or distracted.\n\nDoes that make sense, or should I explain it differently?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to force yourself to do things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I think… sometimes, yeah. Not always in a big way, but there are things I know I used to enjoy, and now I kind of have to talk myself into doing them.\n\nLike, if I do go along with something, I can get through it and I don’t want people to think I’m being difficult. But the starting part feels harder than it should. I don’t know if that counts as forcing myself exactly, but it feels like I’m pushing myself more than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low moods come and go in any kind of pattern, like weekly, monthly, or seasonally?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s really weekly or monthly, at least not that I’ve noticed. It’s been more… just ongoing. I guess it’s been about two years now—maybe a little over, like two years and a couple months.\n\nIt kind of started after the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule began rotating all over the place. I don’t know if that’s the kind of pattern you mean, but that’s when it seemed to settle in and not really let up.\n\nDoctor: Is this low feeling something that you experience for most of the day, almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up already feeling low, like there’s this heavy gray feeling on me before I even get to the clinic. And it kind of stays with me through the day, more or less.\n\nIs that what you mean by most of the day?\n\nDoctor: Do you think of your low periods as separate episodes, or is it more of a continuous feeling?\n\nPatient: It feels more continuous, I think. Not like, “Oh, this week was an episode and then I was back to normal.” It’s more that I wake up already feeling low—kind of that heavy gray feeling I mentioned—and it follows me through most of the day.\n\nI mean, I can still function and get to the clinic, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but… yeah, it’s been more ongoing than separate periods, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this low for most of every day over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. It’s been going on for about two years and maybe a couple months now.\n\nI mean, I can still get through the day, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but the low feeling has been there most days, for most of the day, since around when the clinic staffing changed and my schedule started rotating all over the place. Is that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt low for a shorter amount of time than you do now?\n\nPatient: Not really shorter, no… or at least I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s more like it’s been this same ongoing thing for about two years and two months, since the clinic staffing changed and my schedule started rotating all over the place. I guess there are better moments here and there, but I wouldn’t say there were separate shorter low periods, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not for a full two months, anyway.\n\nThere have been days or maybe a week where I’m a little better, but this has pretty much been going on for about two years and two months, since the clinic staffing changed and my schedule started rotating all over the place. So, no, I don’t think I’ve had a real two-month break from it. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Has your low mood lasted for two years or more without any breaks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s been about two years and two months now, pretty much ongoing, since the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule started rotating all over the place. There hasn’t really been a clean break from it, if that’s what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
